     1→This is a classic challenge when moving from unstructured clinical text to structured, computable logic. The LLM is trying to be too "helpful" by inventing states like `!= present` or `contains confirmed` instead of collapsing them into strict boolean logic, and it's being too greedy by extracting background text as criteria.
     2→
     3→Here is a comprehensive plan to revise your prompts, Pydantic schemas, and LangGraph workflow to enforce strict, accurate extraction and mapping.
     4→
     5→### 1. Schema Revisions (The Guardrails)
     6→
     7→Before we fix the prompts, we need to tighten the Pydantic schemas (likely in `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/schemas/structure.py` or `extraction.py`). The LLM is hallucinating operators because the schema allows it to.
     8→
     9→* **Enforce a Strict Operator Enum:** Restrict the operators strictly to mathematical and boolean comparators. Remove anything like "contains" or "includes".
    10→```python
    11→class RelationalOperator(str, Enum):
    12→    EQUALS = "=="
    13→    NOT_EQUALS = "!="
    14→    GREATER_THAN = ">"
    15→    LESS_THAN = "<"
    16→    GREATER_THAN_OR_EQUAL = ">="
    17→    LESS_THAN_OR_EQUAL = "<="
    18→
    19→```
    20→
    21→
    22→* **Enforce Value Normalization:** For conditions, symptoms, and diagnoses, force the LLM to use boolean representations for presence/absence rather than strings like "present".
    23→* *Instead of:* Entity: "Pregnancy", Operator: "!=", Value: "present"
    24→* *Force:* Entity: "Pregnancy", Operator: "==", Value: "False" (or "True" for required conditions).
    25→
    26→
    27→
    28→### 2. Prompt Revisions (The Instructions)
    29→
    30→We need to update the prompts (specifically `entity_decompose.jinja2` and your extraction prompts) to include explicit rules and **few-shot examples**.
    31→
    32→* **Fixing Noise Extraction (Background Text):**
    33→Update the initial extraction prompt to act as a classifier before extracting.
    34→> **Rule:** *Only extract sentences that represent explicit inclusion or exclusion criteria. Ignore background information, disease definitions, standard care guidelines, or rationale (e.g., "Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee" is background and MUST be ignored).*
    35→
    36→
    37→* **Fixing Value/Operator Mapping:**
    38→Add a "Normalization Guide" directly into `entity_decompose.jinja2`:
    39→> **Normalization Rules:**
    40→
    41→
    42→> 1. For presence/absence of conditions, map to boolean values. Do NOT use "present", "absent", or "confirmed".
    43→> *Example:* "No history of diabetes" -> Entity: "History of diabetes", Operator: "==", Value: "False".
    44→> 2. For laboratory results, extract the exact metric and numeric threshold.
    45→> *Example:* "Platelets > 100,000" -> Entity: "Platelets", Operator: ">", Value: "100000".
    46→> 
    47→> 
    48→
    49→
    50→* **Provide Negative Examples:** LLMs learn exceptionally well from what *not* to do. Include the exact failures you just showed me in the prompt as anti-patterns.
    51→> *Bad Output:* `Entity: Pregnancy, Operator: !=, Value: present`
    52→> *Good Output:* `Entity: Pregnancy, Operator: ==, Value: False`
    53→
    54→
    55→
    56→### 3. LangGraph Workflow Revisions (The Pipeline)
    57→
    58→To ensure the expression tree is built correctly and noise is filtered, we should introduce a validation loop into your graph in `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/graph.py`.
    59→
    60→* **Add a "Filter / Triage" Node:** Before the `extract` or `structure` node, add a lightweight classification node (`is_criterion_node`) that simply returns a boolean: *Is this text a valid eligibility criterion?* If False, route it to an end state or discard it. This prevents background text from ever entering the complex entity decomposition phase.
    61→* **Implement a "Self-Correction" Loop (Reflexion):** Modify the `structure` node so that if the output parser throws a validation error (e.g., because the LLM tried to use the operator "contains confirmed"), it loops back to the LLM with the error message: *"Validation Error: 'contains confirmed' is not a valid operator. You must use '==', '!=', etc. Please try again."*
    62→* **Split the Expression Tree Generation:** Currently, the LLM might be trying to extract entities and build the hierarchical logic (AND/OR) in a single pass. Break this into two nodes:
    63→1. **`extract_entities` node:** Focuses purely on finding the variables (Pregnancy, Platelet count, etc.) and their required states.
    64→2. **`build_expression_tree` node:** Takes the structured entities and focuses solely on the logical relationship between them (AND/OR).
    65→
    66→
    67→
    68→### Summary of the Action Plan:
    69→
    70→1. Update Pydantic Enums to strictly reject non-standard operators.
    71→2. Rewrite `entity_decompose.jinja2` to include explicit normalization rules and your screenshot examples as few-shot guides.
    72→3. Add a `filter_criterion` step to LangGraph to drop background text early.
```

#### 1. `entity_decompose.jinja2`

This is the prompt sent to Gemini to decompose a single criterion sentence into discrete groundable medical entities.

Full content:
```
You are a clinical NLP entity extractor. Given a clinical trial eligibility criterion, extract ONLY the discrete medical concepts that should be grounded to terminology codes.

## Rules
1. Extract the medical ENTITY (not the full sentence). Examples:
   - "eGFR >= 30 mL/min/1.73m2" -> text: "eGFR", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
   - "No history of myocardial infarction" -> text: "myocardial infarction", entity_type: "Condition"
   - "Currently taking calcium channel blockers" -> text: "calcium channel blockers", entity_type: "Medication"
   - "Age 20 to 60 years" -> text: "age", entity_type: "Demographic"
   - "BMI > 30 kg/m2" -> text: "body mass index", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
   - "Coronary artery bypass graft within 3 months" -> text: "coronary artery bypass graft", entity_type: "Procedure"
   - "Potassium >= 5.0 mmol/L" -> text: "potassium", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
   - "LVEF <= 40%" -> text: "left ventricular ejection fraction", entity_type: "Lab_Value"

2. One criterion may yield 0, 1, or multiple entities.
3. Skip non-groundable concepts: consent, willingness, investigator discretion, geographic location, legal capacity.
4. Entity types: Condition | Medication | Lab_Value | Procedure | Demographic | Other
5. The `value` field is NOT used by this tool. Only extract `text` (the medical concept) and `entity_type`.
6. Extract entities even from indication or context-framing criteria...
7. Any clinical scoring system, classification scale, physical status grade, or ordinal assessment is Lab_Value — never "Other".
8. Use "Other" ONLY as a last resort for truly unclassifiable entities.

## Medical Normalization Rules
- Always normalize entity text to the standard medical NOUN form (UMLS, SNOMED, ICD-10)
  - "immunocompromised" → "immunodeficiency"
  - "hypertensive" → "hypertension"
  - "febrile" → "fever"
- When a criterion describes a state resulting from a medical intervention, extract the INTERVENTION as Procedure
  - "immunized against hepatitis B" → "hepatitis B vaccination" (Procedure)
- Do NOT embed demographic qualifiers into clinical entity text

## Compound Concept Rules — DO NOT Over-Decompose
- "tricompartmental knee replacement" → ONE entity
- "spinal muscular atrophy" → ONE entity
- DO separate distinct comorbidities: "Type 2 diabetes with nephropathy" → TWO entities

## Criterion
{% if category %}Category hint: {{ category }}{% endif %}
Text: "{{ criterion_text }}"

Return a JSON object with key "entities" containing an array of {"text": string, "entity_type": string} objects.
```

---

#### 2. `grounding_system.jinja2`

The system prompt given to MedGemma for both candidate evaluation and agentic reasoning.

Full content:
```
You are a medical terminology expert. Your task is to select the best terminology code for a medical entity extracted from a clinical trial eligibility criterion.

You will be given:
- The entity text and type
- The criterion context it came from
- A list of terminology candidates from medical APIs (UMLS, SNOMED, RxNorm, etc.)

Your job: Select the single best match or determine no match exists.

## Confidence Scoring Guidelines
- 0.9-1.0: Exact match
- 0.7-0.8: Close synonym
- 0.5-0.6: Partial/broader match
- 0.0: No valid match

## Rules
1. Prefer exact or near-exact concept matches over broad categories
2. For medications, prefer RxNorm over UMLS when both are available
3. For conditions and procedures, UMLS or SNOMED is appropriate
4. If the entity is a number, unit, or procedural language, set confidence=0.0
5. Always provide reasoning for your selection
6. Output your analysis in plain text — the output will be structured by a downstream system
7. Text overlap is NOT sufficient for a match. The candidate must represent the same CLINICAL MEANING.
8. Use the criterion context to validate candidate relevance.

## Search Strategy (for retry attempts)
- Simplify to the core medical noun
- Drop anatomical qualifiers first, then adjectives
- Expand abbreviations: "HTN" → "hypertension", "MI" → "myocardial infarction"
- Try the generic drug class if a specific brand name fails
```

---

#### 3. `grounding_evaluate.jinja2`

The user-turn prompt MedGemma receives when evaluating candidates for a specific entity.

Full content:
```
Select the best terminology match for the following medical entity.

## Entity
Text: "{{ entity_text }}"
Type: {{ entity_type }}
From criterion: "{{ criterion_context[:200] }}"

## Candidates
{% if candidates %}
{% for c in candidates %}
- Source: {{ c.source_api }} | Code: {{ c.code }} | Term: "{{ c.preferred_term }}"{% if c.semantic_type %} | Semantic: {{ c.semantic_type }}{% endif %} | Score: {{ "%.2f"|format(c.score) }}
{% endfor %}
{% else %}
No candidates found from any terminology API.
{% endif %}

## Instructions
{% if candidates %}
Review the candidates and select the best match...
State:
1. Which candidate best matches the entity (or that none do)
2. Your confidence (0.9-1.0 exact, 0.7-0.8 synonym, 0.5-0.6 partial, 0.0 no match)
3. Your brief reasoning

Be decisive. If a candidate clearly represents the same medical concept, assign confidence ≥ 0.8.
IMPORTANT: Use the criterion context to validate candidates.
{% else %}
No terminology candidates were found. State:
- That no candidates are available
- Confidence: 0.0
- Reasoning: why this entity could not be grounded
{% endif %}
```

---

#### 4. `grounding_reasoning.jinja2`

The agentic retry prompt. MedGemma answers 3 questions when initial grounding fails.

Full content:
```
Evaluate the following medical entity that failed initial terminology grounding.
Answer all three questions below to determine the best retry strategy.

## Entity
Text: "{{ entity_text }}"
Type: {{ entity_type }}
From criterion: "{{ criterion_context[:300] }}"
{% if previous_query and previous_query != entity_text %}
Previous search query: "{{ previous_query }}"
{% endif %}
Attempt: {{ attempt }} of 3

## Three Reasoning Questions

**Q1: Valid medical criterion?**
Is "{{ entity_text }}" actually a valid medical criterion...?
If it is not a valid medical criterion, return should_skip=true.

**Q2: Derived entity?**
Is "{{ entity_text }}" a derived entity that maps to a more standard medical concept?
Examples:
- "age >= 18 years" → the concept is "age"
- "male" or "female" → maps to SNOMED gender concepts
- "BMI > 30" → the concept is "body mass index"

**Q3: Rephrase for better terminology search?**
...
Simplification strategies (try in order):
1. Expand abbreviations/acronyms: "HTN" → "hypertension"
2. Use standard medical terminology: "high blood pressure" → "hypertension"
3. Drop qualifiers to broaden: "severe persistent asthma" → "asthma"
4. Use the core medical noun only: "left ventricular ejection fraction" → "ejection fraction"
```

---

#### 5. `system.jinja2` (extraction system prompt)

The full extraction system prompt sent to Gemini for PDF criteria extraction.

Key sections:
- Instructs Gemini to extract all inclusion/exclusion criteria from a protocol PDF
- Provides composite criteria splitting rules (independent AND/OR conditions split, dependent conditions kept together)
- AutoCriteria decomposition guidance: Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time
- Numeric threshold extraction (comparators: `>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`, `==`, `range`)
- Assertion status classification: PRESENT, ABSENT, HYPOTHETICAL, HISTORICAL, CONDITIONAL
- Full worked examples with JSON output shapes
- Key negation markers: "no history of", "without", "absence of", etc.
- Key conditionality markers: "if", "in case of", "when applicable", "for patients who", "provided that", "unless"
- Page number tracking (1-based)

---

#### 6. `user.jinja2` (extraction user prompt)

```
## Protocol: {{ title }}

Analyze the attached PDF document and extract all inclusion and exclusion criteria...

1. Identify all eligibility criteria sections (inclusion and exclusion)
2. Extract each criterion as a separate item
3. Detect temporal constraints, numeric thresholds, and conditional dependencies
4. Assign assertion status
5. Split independent composite criteria into separate entries

Pay special attention to:
- Criteria in tables or structured lists
- Negation markers ("no history of", "without", "absence of")
- Conditional statements ("if", "for patients who", "when applicable")
- Time-based requirements ("within X months", "at least Y weeks before")
- Independent composite criteria with AND/OR that should be split
```

---

### Pydantic Schema Files (5 files)

**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/schemas/`

---

#### `extraction.py`

Defines the response schema for Gemini structured PDF extraction.

Key models:
- **`AssertionStatus`** (enum): `PRESENT`, `ABSENT`, `HYPOTHETICAL`, `HISTORICAL`, `CONDITIONAL`
- **`TemporalConstraint`**: `duration`, `relation` (within/before/after/at_least), `reference_point`
- **`NumericThreshold`**: `value` (float), `unit` (str), `comparator` (>=, <=, >, <, ==, range), `upper_value` (for range type)
- **`ExtractedCriterion`**: Full criterion object with `text`, `criteria_type` (inclusion/exclusion), `category` (medical_history/lab_values/medications/procedures/other), `temporal_constraint`, `conditions` (list of conditional phrases), `numeric_thresholds`, `assertion_status`, `confidence`, `source_section`, `page_number`
- **`ExtractionResult`**: Wraps `criteria: list[ExtractedCriterion]` + `protocol_summary`

---

#### `grounding.py`

Defines the inter-agent communication schemas between TerminologyRouter and MedGemma.

Key models:
- **`GroundingCandidate`**: `source_api`, `code`, `preferred_term`, `semantic_type`, `score` (0-1)
- **`EntityGroundingResult`**: `entity_text`, `entity_type`, `selected_code`, `selected_system`, `preferred_term`, `confidence`, `candidates`, `reasoning`, `field_mappings` (list of dicts), `criterion_id`, `omop_concept_id`, `reconciliation_status` (agree/disagreement/omop_missing/tooluniverse_missing)
- **`GroundingBatchResult`**: `results` + `errors` (error accumulation pattern)

---

#### `structure.py`

Expression tree schemas for AND/OR/NOT logic storage.

Key models:
- **`LogicNode`**: LLM output tree node. `node_type` (AND/OR/NOT/ATOMIC), `field_mapping_index` (for ATOMIC leaves), `children` (list of LogicNode)
- **`LogicDetectionResponse`**: `root: LogicNode` + `reasoning`
- **`ExpressionNode`**: JSONB storage node. `type`, `atomic_criterion_id`, `entity`, `relation`, `value`, `unit`, `omop_concept_id`, `children`
- **`StructuredCriterionTree`**: `root: ExpressionNode`, `structure_confidence` (llm/fallback), `structure_model`

---

#### `ordinal.py`

Schemas for identifying and proposing ordinal scale config entries.

Key models:
- **`OrdinalValueProposal`**: `grade`, `snomed_code`, `loinc_answer`, `description`
- **`OrdinalScaleProposal`**: `entity_text`, `is_ordinal_scale`, `confidence`, `scale_name` (snake_case), `entity_aliases`, `loinc_code`, `unit_concept_id` (always 8527 = `{score}`), `values`, `reasoning`
- **`OrdinalResolutionResponse`**: `proposals: list[OrdinalScaleProposal]`

---

#### `__init__.py`

Minimal: just a docstring, re-exports nothing directly.

---

### Tool Files — Full Analysis

#### `entity_decomposer.py`

**What it does:** Decomposes a single criterion sentence into 0..N discrete medical entities using Gemini's structured output.

**Key types:**
- `DecomposedEntity`: `text` (medical concept only, e.g. "eGFR"), `entity_type` (Literal: Condition/Medication/Lab_Value/Procedure/Demographic/Other)
- `DecomposedEntityList`: wraps `entities: list[DecomposedEntity]`

**Flow:**
1. `_render_decompose_prompt(criterion_text, category)` → renders `entity_decompose.jinja2`
2. `decompose_entities_from_criterion(criterion_text, category)` → calls `gemini.with_structured_output(DecomposedEntityList)`, returns `list[dict]` with keys `text` and `entity_type`. Returns `[]` on any failure.

**Model:** `GEMINI_MODEL_NAME` env var (default: `gemini-2.5-flash`)

---

#### `field_mapper.py`

**What it does:** Given a grounded entity + criterion text, generates structured AutoCriteria field mappings decomposing the criterion into Entity-Relation-Value-Unit components.

**Relation normalization map:**
```python
_RELATION_MAP = {
    "has": "contains",
    "is": "=",
    "not": "not_contains",
    "==": "=",
    "range": "within",
}
```

**Valid `RelationOperator` values (frontend-accepted set):**
`"="`, `"!="`, `">"`, `">="`, `"<"`, `"<="`, `"within"`, `"not_in_last"`, `"contains"`, `"not_contains"`

**`FieldMappingValue` typed value shapes:**
- `standard`: `{type, value, unit}` — e.g. HbA1c < 7%
- `range`: `{type, min, max, unit}` — e.g. Age 18-65 years
- `temporal`: `{type, duration, unit}` — e.g. within 6 months

**`FieldMappingItem`**: `entity`, `relation` (normalized via validator), `value: FieldMappingValue`, `unit`, `value_concept_id`, `value_concept_system`

**`FieldMappingResponse`**: `mappings: list[FieldMappingItem]`

**`generate_field_mappings(entity, criterion_text)`** output dict keys:
`entity`, `relation`, `value` (FieldMappingValue.model_dump()), `entity_code`, `entity_system`, `omop_concept_id`, `entity_type`, `value_concept_id`, `value_concept_system`

The prompt (inline, not a template) explicitly lists the valid relation operators and the 3 value type shapes with JSON examples.

---

#### `structure_builder.py`

**What it does:** Phase 2 — builds AND/OR/NOT expression trees from field mappings.

**`_extract_value_fields(fm)`:** Handles both legacy flat string values and typed `FieldMappingValue` dicts. For range type, formats as `"min-max"`. For temporal, returns duration. For standard, returns value.

**`_parse_value(raw_value)`:** Tries float conversion; returns `(float, None)` or `(None, str)`.

**`_ENTITY_TYPE_TO_DOMAIN`** mapping:
```python
{
    "Condition": "condition",
    "Medication": "drug",
    "Lab_Value": "measurement",
    "Procedure": "procedure",
    "Demographic": "demographics",
    "Other": "observation",
}
```

**`_create_atomic_from_mapping(fm, ...)`:** Creates `AtomicCriterion` DB record. Applies ordinal-first resolution: `normalize_ordinal_value(value_str, entity_text)` → if matched, sets `value_concept_id` and `unit_concept_id=8527`. Falls back to `normalize_value(value_text)` for non-numeric text values.

**`detect_logic_structure(criterion_text, field_mappings)`:** Calls Gemini to detect AND/OR/NOT/ATOMIC tree. Skips if only 1 mapping. Validates all `field_mapping_index` values are in-range.

**`build_expression_tree(...)`** two-pass approach:
1. LLM pass: call `detect_logic_structure()` → if success, build tree from `_build_tree_from_logic()`
2. Fallback: single mapping → single ATOMIC node; multiple mappings → wrap all in AND composite

---

#### `gemini_extractor.py`

**What it does:** Uploads PDF to Gemini File API, calls Gemini with `ExtractionResult` as structured output schema, returns JSON string.

**Retry:** 3 attempts, exponential backoff (2-10s). Circuit breaker via `gemini_breaker`.

**Local mode:** When `LOCAL_EXTRACTION_ENABLED=true`, routes through `InferenceGateway` (Ollama path) instead.

**Templates used:** `system.jinja2` + `user.jinja2` rendered via `inference.factory.render_prompts()`.

---

#### `medgemma_decider.py`

**What it does:** Two functions: `medgemma_decide()` (select best candidate) and `agentic_reasoning_loop()` (determine retry strategy on failure).

**Two-model architecture:**
1. MedGemma: free-form medical reasoning (uses `grounding_system.jinja2` + `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` or `grounding_reasoning.jinja2`)
2. Gemini: structures MedGemma's raw text output into `GroundingDecision` or `AgenticReasoningResult`

**`GroundingDecision`**: `selected_code`, `selected_system`, `preferred_term`, `confidence` (0-1), `reasoning`

**`AgenticReasoningResult`**: `should_skip`, `is_derived`, `derived_term`, `rephrased_query`, `gemini_suggestion`, `reasoning`

**`agentic_reasoning_loop(entity, criterion_context, router, attempt)`:** Answers MedGemma's 3 questions. Gemini can also add its own `gemini_suggestion` when structuring the output.

---

#### `ordinal_resolver.py`

**What it does:** Identifies unrecognized clinical ordinal scales (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, ASA, GCS, APACHE II, MELD, mRS, SOFA, Child-Pugh) and proposes YAML config entries for human review.

**Threshold:** Only proposals with `is_ordinal_scale=True` and `confidence >= 0.7` are returned.

**Key distinction enforced by prompt:** Ordinal scales (discrete grades with clinical meaning, unit=`{score}`) vs continuous measurements (lab values with physical units).

---

#### `tooluniverse_client.py`

**What it does:** Singleton wrapper around ToolUniverse SDK for all 6 terminology systems.

**System-to-tool map:**
```python
{
    "umls": "umls_search_concepts",
    "snomed": "snomed_search_concepts",
    "icd10": "ICD10_search_codes",
    "loinc": "LOINC_search_tests",
    "rxnorm": "RxNorm_get_drug_names",
    "hpo": "HPO_search_terms",
}
```

**Argument shapes per system:**
- umls/snomed: `{query, pageSize: min(max, 25)}`
- icd10: `{query, limit: min(max, 100)}`
- loinc: `{terms, max_results: min(max, 20)}`
- rxnorm: `{drug_name}` (returns single best match, not a list)
- hpo: `{query, max_results: min(max, 50)}`

**Response parsing per system:**
- umls/snomed: `data.result.results[].{ui, name, rootSource}` — score = `max(1.0 - i*0.1, 0.5)`
- icd10: `data.results[].{code, name}`
- loinc: `results[].{LOINC_NUM, LONG_COMMON_NAME}`
- rxnorm: single `{rxcui, drug_name}` at score=0.9
- hpo: `data[].{id, name}`

**Caching:** 2-layer: exact-match TTLCache (300s, 1000 items) + prefix-aware cache (reuses "hyp" results for "hype" queries by filtering preferred_term).

**Sentence extraction:** Queries >= 4 words or >= 40 chars are pre-processed to strip clinical preamble ("patients must have", "history of", etc.) before API calls.

**Circuit breaker + retry:** `@tu_breaker` (outer) wraps `@retry` (inner, 3 attempts, exponential 2-10s). Transient markers: `"502"`, `"503"`, `"Service Unavailable"`, `"timeout"`.

---

#### `terminology_router.py`

**What it does:** Loads `routing.yaml` and routes entities to the correct set of terminology APIs.

**`routing.yaml` config:**
```yaml
routing_rules:
  Medication: [rxnorm, umls]
  Condition: [icd10, snomed, umls]
  Lab_Value: [loinc, umls]
  Biomarker: [hpo, umls]
  Procedure: [umls, snomed]
  Phenotype: [hpo, umls]
  Demographic: [umls, snomed]
  Consent: {skip: true}
  Other: []   # → expert_review routing
```

**Acronym detection (`_is_likely_acronym`):** <= 6 chars, all uppercase, no spaces → UMLS always added.

---

#### `ground.py` (the orchestrating node)

**Pipeline position:** ingest → extract → parse → **ground** → persist

**Key functions:**
- `_reconcile_dual_grounding(result, omop_result)`: Compares TerminologyRouter (ToolUniverse) result vs OMOP mapper result using `fuzz.token_set_ratio >= 80` for agreement. Sets `reconciliation_status`: agree / disagreement / omop_missing / tooluniverse_missing.
- `_ground_entity_with_retry(entity, router, criterion_text)`: 3-attempt agentic loop. Initial grounding → if confidence < 0.5 and no code → ask MedGemma 3 questions → retry with `rephrased_query` or `derived_term` or `gemini_suggestion`. After 3 failures → `[expert_review]` prefix in reasoning.
- `_ground_categorical_values(mappings)`: Post-processes field mappings — for `standard`-type non-numeric values, calls `normalize_value(val_text)` to resolve `value_concept_id`.
- `_ground_entity_parallel(...)`: Runs TerminologyRouter + OMOP mapper in parallel (`asyncio.gather`), then generates field mappings and categorical value grounding. Semaphore(4) for concurrency control.
- `ground_node(state)`: Main node. Skips `Consent` entities pre-emptively. Respects `DEV_MAX_ENTITIES` / `PIPELINE_MAX_ENTITIES` env vars. MedGemma warmup before batch. Batch-writes audit logs after all entities processed.

---

#### `unit_normalizer.py`

**What it does:** Pure DB lookup (no LLM). UCUM unit resolution and OMOP concept_id lookup for units and categorical values.

**`ORDINAL_SCALE_ALIASES`** dict: maps aliases like `"ecog ps"`, `"kps"`, `"nyha class"`, `"asa physical status"`, `"who performance status"`, `"zubrod score"`, etc. to canonical keys `"ecog"`, `"karnofsky"`, `"nyha"`, `"asa"`.

**`normalize_unit(unit_text)`:** → `(ucum_code, omop_unit_concept_id)` via `_lookup_ucum_unit()` from OMOP DB. lru_cache(512).

**`normalize_value(value_text)`:** → `(normalized_text, omop_value_concept_id)` via `_lookup_value_concept()`. lru_cache(128).

**`normalize_ordinal_value(value_text, entity_text)`:** 3-strategy entity matching (exact alias, entity-in-alias substring, alias-in-entity substring). Strips ordinal prefixes (`grade`, `stage`, `class`, `score`, `level`). Returns `(value_concept_id, unit_concept_id=8527)` tuple or `None` if entity doesn't match any scale.

**`_ORDINAL_UNIT_CONCEPT_ID = 8527`** — OMOP UCUM concept for `{score}`.

---

#### `gemini_utils.py`

Shared helper for creating structured LLM clients. Tries Gemini first (GOOGLE_API_KEY), falls back to Ollama (OLLAMA_BASE_URL, OLLAMA_MODEL default `"gemma2:9b"`). `parse_structured_output()` handles both dict and already-parsed model instances from LangChain.

---

### Test Files

#### `scripts/test_grounding_snippets.py`

Script for live pipeline evaluation. Two modes:
1. `ground_snippet(snippet)` — decomposes criterion via entity_decomposer first, then grounds
2. `ground_snippet_with_known_entities(snippet)` — uses pre-defined golden entities from JSON (skips extraction), runs full grounding + field_mapping, compares `got_relation`/`got_value` against `expected_relation`/`expected_value` for manual comparison (no rigid pass/fail on value/relation). Code match is binary.

Output: MATCH/MISS/FAIL per entity with color-coded symbols, plus summary accuracy.

#### `tests/e2e/test_grounding_snippets.py`

Pytest e2e suite. `@pytest.mark.e2e` marker. Parametrized over all snippet indices (counts loaded dynamically from JSON).

**Structural tests (all snippets):**
- `test_grounding_snippet_has_entities` — at least one entity per snippet
- `test_grounding_snippet_entity_fields` — required fields: `entity_name`, `system`, `code`, `relation`, `value`
- `test_grounding_snippet_system_is_valid` — system in `{UMLS, SNOMED, ICD10, LOINC, RxNorm, HPO}`
- `test_grounding_snippet_codes_are_cui_format` — all codes start with `"C"` followed by digits
- `test_grounding_snippet_relations_are_valid` — relation (after normalization) in `{<, <=, >, >=, =, !=, contains, not_contains, within, ==}`

**Named golden tests:**
- Snippet 0: Serum creatinine → `C0201975`, relation `>`
- Snippet 1: eGFR → `C0858118`, relation `<`
- Snippet 2: Body Weight → `C0005910`, Body Mass Index → `C1305855`
- Snippet 3: Ankylosing Spondylitis → `C0038013`
- Snippet 4: Parkinson's Disease → `C0030567`, GBA gene mutation → `C4225361`
- Snippet 5: ASA physical status → `C0450990`

### Summary: How Entities Flow Through the Pipeline

1. **Extraction** (`gemini_extractor.py`): PDF → Gemini (File API) → `ExtractionResult` (list of `ExtractedCriterion` with text, numeric_thresholds, assertion_status, temporal_constraint)

2. **Entity Decomposition** (`entity_decomposer.py`): Each criterion text → Gemini (`entity_decompose.jinja2`) → `DecomposedEntity` list with (text, entity_type)

3. **Routing** (`terminology_router.py` + `routing.yaml`): entity_type → API list. Consent skipped. Other → expert_review. Acronyms always get UMLS added.

4. **Terminology Lookup** (`tooluniverse_client.py`): Each API queried via ToolUniverse SDK → `GroundingCandidate` list with (source_api, code, preferred_term, semantic_type, score)

5. **MedGemma Decision** (`medgemma_decider.py`): `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` → MedGemma free text → Gemini structures it into `GroundingDecision`. If confidence < 0.5: `grounding_reasoning.jinja2` → agentic 3-question loop → retry up to 3 total attempts.

6. **OMOP Dual Grounding** (`ground.py`): TerminologyRouter + OMOP mapper run in parallel per entity. Fuzzy reconciliation (token_set_ratio >= 80) → agree/disagreement/omop_missing/tooluniverse_missing status.

7. **Field Mapping** (`field_mapper.py`): Grounded entity + criterion text → Gemini inline prompt → `FieldMappingResponse` (list of `FieldMappingItem` with entity, relation, typed value, unit). Categorical values post-processed via `normalize_value()`.

8. **Ordinal Normalization** (`unit_normalizer.py`): Value strings for known ordinal scales (ECOG, NYHA, ASA, Karnofsky) → OMOP value_concept_id + unit_concept_id=8527.

9. **Expression Tree** (`structure_builder.py`): Field mappings + criterion text → Gemini logic detection (AND/OR/NOT/ATOMIC tree) → `AtomicCriterion` + `CompositeCriterion` + `CriterionRelationship` DB records → `StructuredCriterionTree` JSONB stored in criteria table.

     1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""Run grounding test snippets through the live pipeline and compare to golden values.
     3→
     4→Usage:
     5→    set -a && source .env && set +a
     6→    uv run python scripts/test_grounding_snippets.py
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import json
    13→from pathlib import Path
    14→
    15→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16→# Load snippets
    17→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    18→
    19→SNIPPETS_PATH = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "tests" / "e2e" / "test_snippets.json"
    20→data = json.loads(SNIPPETS_PATH.read_text())
    21→grounding_snippets = data["grounding_test_snippets"]
    22→
    23→
    24→# Relation normalization (same as pipeline)
    25→_REL_NORM = {"==": "=", "is": "=", "has": "contains", "not": "not_contains"}
    26→
    27→
    28→def norm_rel(r: str) -> str:
    29→    return _REL_NORM.get(r, r)
    30→
    31→
    32→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    33→# Core grounding logic (extracted from ground_node)
    34→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    35→
    36→
    37→async def ground_snippet(snippet: dict) -> dict:
    38→    """Ground a single snippet through TerminologyRouter + MedGemma + OMOP."""
    39→    from protocol_processor.nodes.ground import (
    40→        _ground_categorical_values,
    41→        _ground_entity_with_retry,
    42→        _reconcile_dual_grounding,
    43→    )
    44→    from protocol_processor.tools.field_mapper import generate_field_mappings
    45→    from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import lookup_omop_concept
    46→    from protocol_processor.tools.terminology_router import (
    47→        TerminologyRouter,
    48→        _is_likely_acronym,
    49→    )
    50→
    51→    router = TerminologyRouter()
    52→    results = []
    53→
    54→    # We don't have pre-extracted entities — we need to extract them first.
    55→    # Use Gemini to decompose the snippet into entities, then ground each one.
    56→    from protocol_processor.tools.entity_decomposer import (
    57→        decompose_entities_from_criterion,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→    entities = await decompose_entities_from_criterion(snippet["snippet_text"], None)
    61→
    62→    for ent in entities:
    63→        entity_text = ent.get("text", ent.get("entity", ""))
    64→        entity_type = ent.get("entity_type", "Condition")
    65→
    66→        entity_dict = {
    67→            "text": entity_text,
    68→            "entity_type": entity_type,
    69→            "criterion_text": snippet["snippet_text"],
    70→        }
    71→
    72→        try:
    73→            acronym = _is_likely_acronym(entity_text)
    74→
    75→            # Parallel: TerminologyRouter + OMOP
    76→            tu_task = _ground_entity_with_retry(
    77→                entity_dict, router, snippet["snippet_text"]
    78→            )
    79→            omop_task = lookup_omop_concept(
    80→                entity_text, entity_type, is_acronym=acronym
    81→            )
    82→            (result, _attempts), omop_result = await asyncio.gather(tu_task, omop_task)
    83→
    84→            result = _reconcile_dual_grounding(result, omop_result)
    85→
    86→            # Field mappings
    87→            field_mappings = await generate_field_mappings(
    88→                result, snippet["snippet_text"]
    89→            )
    90→            if field_mappings:
    91→                field_mappings = _ground_categorical_values(field_mappings)
    92→            result.field_mappings = field_mappings or None
    93→
    94→            results.append(
    95→                {
    96→                    "entity_name": result.preferred_term or result.entity_text,
    97→                    "entity_text": result.entity_text,
    98→                    "entity_type": entity_type,
    99→                    "system": result.selected_system,
   100→                    "code": result.selected_code,
   101→                    "confidence": result.confidence,
   102→                    "omop_id": result.omop_concept_id,
   103→                    "reconciliation": result.reconciliation_status,
   104→                    "field_mappings": result.field_mappings,
   105→                }
   106→            )
   107→        except Exception as e:
   108→            results.append(
   109→                {
   110→                    "entity_name": entity_text,
   111→                    "entity_text": entity_text,
   112→                    "entity_type": entity_type,
   113→                    "error": str(e),
   114→                }
   115→            )
   116→
   117→    return {"snippet_text": snippet["snippet_text"], "grounded_entities": results}
   118→
   119→
   120→def _value_from_field_mapping(fm: dict) -> str | None:
   121→    """Extract display value from a field_mapping for reporting."""
   122→    val = fm.get("value")
   123→    if isinstance(val, dict):
   124→        return val.get("value") or val.get("raw")
   125→    return str(val) if val is not None else None
   126→
   127→
   128→async def ground_snippet_with_known_entities(snippet: dict) -> dict:
   129→    """Ground a snippet using its pre-defined golden entities (skip extraction)."""
   130→    from protocol_processor.nodes.ground import (
   131→        _ground_categorical_values,
   132→        _ground_entity_with_retry,
   133→        _reconcile_dual_grounding,
   134→    )
   135→    from protocol_processor.tools.field_mapper import generate_field_mappings
   136→    from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import lookup_omop_concept
   137→    from protocol_processor.tools.terminology_router import (
   138→        TerminologyRouter,
   139→        _is_likely_acronym,
   140→    )
   141→
   142→    router = TerminologyRouter()
   143→    results = []
   144→
   145→    for expected_entity in snippet["entities"]:
   146→        entity_text = expected_entity["entity_name"]
   147→        # Guess entity_type from context
   148→        entity_type = (
   149→            "Lab_Value"
   150→            if expected_entity["relation"] in ("<", ">", "<=", ">=")
   151→            else "Condition"
   152→        )
   153→
   154→        entity_dict = {
   155→            "text": entity_text,
   156→            "entity_type": entity_type,
   157→            "criterion_text": snippet["snippet_text"],
   158→        }
   159→
   160→        try:
   161→            acronym = _is_likely_acronym(entity_text)
   162→
   163→            tu_task = _ground_entity_with_retry(
   164→                entity_dict, router, snippet["snippet_text"]
   165→            )
   166→            omop_task = lookup_omop_concept(
   167→                entity_text, entity_type, is_acronym=acronym
   168→            )
   169→            (result, _attempts), omop_result = await asyncio.gather(tu_task, omop_task)
   170→
   171→            result = _reconcile_dual_grounding(result, omop_result)
   172→
   173→            # Generate field_mappings for value/relation manual comparison
   174→            field_mappings = await generate_field_mappings(
   175→                result, snippet["snippet_text"]
   176→            )
   177→            if field_mappings:
   178→                field_mappings = _ground_categorical_values(field_mappings)
   179→
   180→            got_rel = got_val = None
   181→            if field_mappings:
   182→                first = field_mappings[0]
   183→                got_rel = first.get("relation")
   184→                got_val = _value_from_field_mapping(first)
   185→
   186→            results.append(
   187→                {
   188→                    "entity_name": result.preferred_term or result.entity_text,
   189→                    "entity_text": result.entity_text,
   190→                    "system": result.selected_system,
   191→                    "code": result.selected_code,
   192→                    "confidence": result.confidence,
   193→                    "omop_id": result.omop_concept_id,
   194→                    "reconciliation": result.reconciliation_status,
   195→                    "expected_code": expected_entity["code"],
   196→                    "expected_system": expected_entity["system"],
   197→                    "expected_relation": expected_entity.get("relation"),
   198→                    "expected_value": expected_entity.get("value"),
   199→                    "got_relation": got_rel,
   200→                    "got_value": got_val,
   201→                    "code_match": (
   202→                        result.selected_code == expected_entity["code"]
   203→                        if result.selected_code
   204→                        else False
   205→                    ),
   206→                }
   207→            )
   208→        except Exception as e:
   209→            results.append(
   210→                {
   211→                    "entity_name": entity_text,
   212→                    "error": str(e),
   213→                    "expected_code": expected_entity["code"],
   214→                    "expected_relation": expected_entity.get("relation"),
   215→                    "expected_value": expected_entity.get("value"),
   216→                    "got_relation": None,
   217→                    "got_value": None,
   218→                    "code_match": False,
   219→                }
   220→            )
   221→
   222→    return {"snippet_text": snippet["snippet_text"], "grounded_entities": results}
   223→
   224→
   225→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   226→# Pretty-print results
   227→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   228→
   229→
   230→def print_results(all_results: list[dict]) -> None:
   231→    total_entities = 0
   232→    matched = 0
   233→    grounded = 0
   234→
   235→    for i, res in enumerate(all_results):
   236→        print(f"\n{'=' * 80}")
   237→        snippet = res["snippet_text"]
   238→        print(f"Snippet {i + 1}: {snippet[:100]}{'...' if len(snippet) > 100 else ''}")
   239→        print(f"{'=' * 80}")
   240→
   241→        for ent in res["grounded_entities"]:
   242→            total_entities += 1
   243→            code = ent.get("code")
   244→            expected = ent.get("expected_code", "?")
   245→            match = ent.get("code_match", False)
   246→            conf = ent.get("confidence", 0)
   247→            err = ent.get("error")
   248→
   249→            if code:
   250→                grounded += 1
   251→            if match:
   252→                matched += 1
   253→
   254→            status = "MATCH" if match else ("MISS" if code else "FAIL")
   255→            icon = (
   256→                "\033[32m✓\033[0m"
   257→                if match
   258→                else ("\033[33m≈\033[0m" if code else "\033[31m✗\033[0m")
   259→            )
   260→
   261→            if err:
   262→                print(f"  {icon} [{status}] {ent['entity_name']}")
   263→                print(f"      ERROR: {err}")
   264→            else:
   265→                name = ent.get("entity_name", "?")
   266→                system = ent.get("system", "?")
   267→                omop = ent.get("omop_id", "—")
   268→                recon = ent.get("reconciliation", "—")
   269→                print(f"  {icon} [{status}] {name}")
   270→                print(
   271→                    f"      Got:      {system}:{code}  (conf={conf:.2f}, omop={omop}, recon={recon})"
   272→                )
   273→                print(f"      Expected: {ent.get('expected_system', '?')}:{expected}")
   274→                # Value/relation for manual comparison (no rigid pass/fail)
   275→                exp_rel = ent.get("expected_relation")
   276→                exp_val = ent.get("expected_value")
   277→                got_rel = ent.get("got_relation")
   278→                got_val = ent.get("got_value")
   279→                if exp_rel is not None or exp_val is not None or got_rel is not None or got_val is not None:
   280→                    print(
   281→                        f"      Value:    expected relation={exp_rel!r} value={exp_val!r}  |  got relation={got_rel!r} value={got_val!r}"
   282→                    )
   283→
   284→    print(f"\n{'=' * 80}")
   285→    print("SUMMARY")
   286→    print(f"{'=' * 80}")
   287→    print(f"  Total entities:  {total_entities}")
   288→    print(f"  Grounded:        {grounded}/{total_entities}")
   289→    print(f"  Exact code match: {matched}/{total_entities}")
   290→    print(
   291→        f"  Accuracy:        {matched / total_entities * 100:.0f}%"
   292→        if total_entities
   293→        else "  Accuracy:        N/A"
   294→    )
   295→
   296→
   297→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   298→# Main
   299→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   300→
   301→
   302→async def main() -> None:
   303→    print("Grounding Test Snippets — Live Pipeline Evaluation")
   304→    print(f"Running {len(grounding_snippets)} snippets with known entities...\n")
   305→
   306→    results = []
   307→    for i, snippet in enumerate(grounding_snippets):
   308→        entity_count = len(snippet["entities"])
   309→        print(
   310→            f"[{i + 1}/{len(grounding_snippets)}] "
   311→            f"Grounding {entity_count} entities from: "
   312→            f"{snippet['snippet_text'][:70]}..."
   313→        )
   314→        result = await ground_snippet_with_known_entities(snippet)
   315→        results.append(result)
   316→
   317→    print_results(results)
   318→
   319→
   320→if __name__ == "__main__":
   321→    asyncio.run(main())
   322→
```

    28→# Mapping from legacy/LLM relation operators to the frontend's RelationOperator set
    29→_RELATION_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    30→    "has": "contains",
    31→    "is": "=",
    32→    "not": "not_contains",
    33→    "==": "=",
    34→    "range": "within",
    35→}
    36→
    37→
    38→def _normalize_relation(rel: str) -> str:
    39→    """Normalize a relation operator to the frontend's accepted set.
    40→
    41→    Maps legacy operators (has, is, not, ==, range) to the standard set:
    42→    =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within, not_in_last, contains, not_contains.
    43→    """
    44→    return _RELATION_MAP.get(rel, rel)
    45→
    46→
    47→class FieldMappingValue(BaseModel):
    48→    """Typed value object for field mappings with type discriminator.
    49→
    50→    Supports three value shapes:
    51→    - standard: single value + unit (e.g. HbA1c < 7%)
    52→    - range: min/max + unit (e.g. Age 18-65 years)
    53→    - temporal: duration + unit (e.g. within 6 months)
    54→    """
    55→
    56→    type: Literal["standard", "range", "temporal"] = Field(
    57→        description="Value type discriminator"
    58→    )
    59→    value: str | None = Field(
    60→        default=None, description="Value for standard type (e.g. '7')"
    61→    )
    62→    unit: str | None = Field(
    63→        default=None, description="Unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'months')"
    64→    )
    65→    min: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Minimum value for range type")
    66→    max: str | None = Field(default=None, description="Maximum value for range type")
    67→    duration: str | None = Field(
    68→        default=None, description="Duration value for temporal type"
    69→    )
    70→
    71→
    72→# The set of valid relation operators accepted by the frontend
    73→RelationOperator = Literal[
    74→    "=", "!=", ">", ">=", "<", "<=", "within", "not_in_last", "contains", "not_contains"
    75→]
    76→
    77→
    78→class FieldMappingItem(BaseModel):
    79→    """A single AutoCriteria field mapping decomposition.
    80→
    81→    Per the AutoCriteria pattern, each criterion is decomposed into
    82→    separate Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, and Time components.
    83→    """
    84→
    85→    entity: str = Field(description="The medical entity name (e.g. 'HbA1c')")
    86→    relation: RelationOperator = Field(
    87→        description="The logical operator/relation (e.g. '<', '>', '=', 'contains')"
    88→    )
    89→    value: FieldMappingValue = Field(
    90→        description="Typed value object with type discriminator"
    91→    )
    92→    unit: str | None = Field(
    93→        default=None,
    94→        description="Optional unit of measurement (e.g. '%', 'mg/dL', 'years')",
    95→    )
    96→    value_concept_id: str | None = Field(
    97→        default=None,
    98→        description="OMOP concept ID for categorical values",
    99→    )
   100→    value_concept_system: str | None = Field(
   101→        default=None,
   102→        description="Terminology system for value_concept_id (e.g. 'SNOMED', 'OMOP')",
   103→    )
   104→
   105→    @field_validator("relation", mode="before")
   106→    @classmethod
   107→    def normalize_relation(cls, v: str) -> str:
   108→        """Normalize LLM-generated relation operators before Literal validation."""
   109→        if isinstance(v, str):
   110→            return _normalize_relation(v)
   111→        return v
   112→

   114→class FieldMappingResponse(BaseModel):
   115→    """Gemini structured output for field mappings."""
   116→
   117→    mappings: list[FieldMappingItem] = Field(

   122→
   123→async def generate_field_mappings(
   124→    entity: EntityGroundingResult,
   125→    criterion_text: str,
   126→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   127→    """Generate suggested field mappings for a grounded entity.
   128→
   129→    Uses Gemini to decompose the criterion text into AutoCriteria field mappings:
   130→    Entity, Operator, Value, Unit, Time components for each discrete condition.
   131→
   132→    This is a best-effort suggestion — reviewer can edit in the UI. Errors
   133→    are logged and an empty list returned (not propagated as failures).
   134→
   135→    Args:
   136→        entity: Grounded EntityGroundingResult with code and preferred term.
   137→        criterion_text: Full criterion text for context.
   138→
   139→    Returns:
   140→        List of field mapping dicts with keys: entity, relation, value,
   141→        entity_code, entity_system, omop_concept_id, entity_type.
   142→        Empty list if generation fails.
   143→    """
   144→    if not criterion_text:
   145→        return []
   146→
   147→    structured_llm = create_structured_llm(FieldMappingResponse)
   148→    if structured_llm is None:
   149→        return []
   150→
   151→    try:
   152→        # Build a context-rich prompt for field mapping generation
   153→        grounded_term = entity.preferred_term or entity.entity_text
   154→        code_context = ""
   155→        if entity.selected_code and entity.selected_system:
   156→            system = entity.selected_system.upper()
   157→            code_context = f"(grounded to {system} code: {entity.selected_code})"
   158→
   159→        prompt = (
   160→            "You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Decompose the"
   161→            " following criterion into structured AutoCriteria field"
   162→            " mappings using the Entity-Relation-Value-Unit pattern.\n\n"
   163→            f"Medical entity: {grounded_term} {code_context}\n"
   164→            f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n\n"
   165→            "Instructions:\n"
   166→            "- Extract each discrete measurement, threshold, or condition"
   167→            " as a separate mapping\n"
   168→            "- entity: the specific measurement or concept"
   169→            " (e.g. 'HbA1c', 'Age', 'eGFR')\n"
   170→            "- relation: MUST be one of: =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within,"
   171→            " not_in_last, contains, not_contains\n"
   172→            '- value: a typed object with a "type" discriminator:\n'
   173→            '  - Standard: {"type": "standard", "value": "7", "unit": "%"}\n'
   174→            '  - Range: {"type": "range", "min": "18", "max": "65",'
   175→            ' "unit": "years"}\n'
   176→            '  - Temporal: {"type": "temporal", "duration": "6",'
   177→            ' "unit": "months"}\n'
   178→            "- Create one mapping per discrete condition in the criterion\n"
   179→            "- If no clear measurement exists, create one mapping with"
   180→            " relation='contains',"
   181→            ' value={"type": "standard", "value": "confirmed", "unit": ""}'
   182→        )
   183→
   184→        from protocol_processor.tracing import llm_span
   185→
   186→        with llm_span("gemini_field_mapping") as llm:
   187→            llm.set_request(prompt)
   188→            result = await structured_llm.ainvoke(prompt)
   189→            llm.set_response(str(result))
   190→
   191→        response = parse_structured_output(result, FieldMappingResponse)
   192→
   193→        mappings = [
   194→            {
   195→                "entity": m.entity,
   196→                "relation": m.relation,
   197→                "value": m.value.model_dump(exclude_none=True),
   198→                "entity_code": entity.selected_code,
   199→                "entity_system": entity.selected_system,
   200→                "omop_concept_id": entity.omop_concept_id,
   201→                "entity_type": entity.entity_type,
   202→                "value_concept_id": m.value_concept_id,
   203→                "value_concept_system": m.value_concept_system,
   204→            }
   205→            for m in response.mappings
   206→        ]
   207→
   208→        logger.info(
   209→            "Generated %d field mapping(s) for entity '%s'",
   210→            len(mappings),
   211→            entity.entity_text[:50],
   212→        )
   213→        return mappings
   214→
   215→    except Exception as e:
   216→        logger.warning(

   219→            e,
   220→            exc_info=True,
